The 256-descriptor bound is per tree; the start's sweep removes leftovers concurrently, which matters only with thousands of them.

### R-G6

**Redesign briefs around the task** · Phase P2 · Effort M · Status: open

- **Resolves:** [AGT-50](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-50-briefs-are-mostly-controller-protocol-boilerplate-the-task-itself-is-a-small-fraction), [AGT-53](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-53-the-parents-final-message-is-inlined-verbatim-duplicating-the-handoff-and-breaking-brief-structure), [AGT-54](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-54-structured-output-instructions-are-scattered-across-6-modules-and-conflict-for-some-roles), [AGT-56](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-56-per-run-context-artifacts-are-oversized-and-copied-on-every-run), [AGT-57](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-57-briefs-inherit-links-into-other-runs-scratch-and-plan-paths-that-later-expire), [AGT-58](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-58-the-scope-section-repeats-the-goal-three-times-and-dumps-internal-id-json)
- **Change:** Goal and acceptance criteria first; controller protocol in one module with role-specific structured-output instructions that do not conflict; reference the parent run via the handoff instead of inlining its final message; stop copying oversized context artifacts per run; no links into other runs' expiring scratch; scope section states the goal once.
- **Done when:** Median brief size and the goal's share of it are tracked; goal share rises substantially from <5%.
- **LIVE-26, fixed 2026-09-30:** the brief said CARGO_TARGET_DIR is the worktree's build cache and to keep build outputs there, while the daemon-run launchers (`ct-native`, the Cargo launcher, `ct-check`) build in a directory the daemon chooses, so WI-03's agent stopped to ask for a corrected manifest. The pinned-environment paragraph now says those builds use their own directory, that receipts do not depend on it, that CARGO_TARGET_DIR is for Cargo commands the agent runs directly, and not to edit the manifest or override it. Test: `server-execution-receipt-gates.test.ts` (the brief says so; it failed before the change).
  - **Independent review of 1016bf6 (2026-09-30, isolated worktree).** No HIGH or MEDIUM. Fixed (mutations B1, B2, killed): *LOW,* the sentence was false for a run with no worktree cache, whose CARGO_TARGET_DIR is the manifest's own `scratch/target`, and *LOW,* "CARGO_TARGET_DIR is for Cargo commands you run directly" described almost nothing, since the launcher is first on PATH and runs its non-build commands locally with the manifest's target: the paragraph now says the launcher's builds and CraftingTable's checks use a directory CraftingTable chooses, and the launcher's other Cargo commands the manifest's (the run's `scratch/target`), whatever CARGO_TARGET_DIR says. *LOW,* the Temporary files section still said to keep builds in the worktree cache; for a run with the launchers it now adds that they use their own directories. *NIT,* `ct-act` is listed. *NIT, disposition:* "do not override the build directory" is advice (`--target-dir` is not refused for dirty-tree builds, which no gate accepts); the test covers the run with a worktree cache only.

### R-G7

**Stop cold-building Rust on every step** · Phase P1 · Effort M · Status: partial (0fc17d2; live measurement after deploy)

- **Resolves:** [AGT-05](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-05-per-run-cargo_target_dir-forces-a-cold-rust-build-on-every-step-768-gb-written-and-deleted-in-10-days)
- **Change:** Share a Cargo target directory per worktree (or per repository with a lock) across the steps of a cycle, with the ADR-039 cleanup applied when the worktree is merged/removed.
- **Done when:** Cache removal volume per day drops by an order of magnitude from the 768 GB/10-day baseline. *(Restated 2026-09-24, see below.)*
- **Progress (2026-09-24):**
  - **One Cargo target per worktree.** Each worktree gets one Cargo target directory, `<runs root>/worktree-caches/<worktree id>`, registered in `worktree_build_caches` (migration 0030) at the worktree's first launch. Every run in the worktree gets it as `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, through the launch request's new `buildCacheDirectory`, so later steps build incrementally.
    - Cargo creates the directory on its first build, so worktrees that never build Rust leave nothing.
    - The brief now names the shared cache and asks agents to keep builds there instead of making their own. Eight of the 101 removed caches had been agent-made target directories.
    - Pinned-evidence and historical-baseline builds keep per-run targets, because their provenance is per run.
  - **Cleanup (ADR-039, amended).** The shared cache is removed on the storage worker's tick once the worktree is merged or removed and no run in it is live.
    - Removal is under the worktree mutation guard, with the registered path and device checked and links never followed.
    - It is audited as `storage.cleaned` with the worktree id. The Storage page's scan counts shared caches.
  - **Tests:** launch sharing and brief text (`server-execution-runs.test.ts`), cleanup only after merge and never through a link (`storage-management.test.ts`), both adapters' `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`.
  - **Measured on the 2026-09-23 snapshot's cleanup audit (2026-09-13 to 2026-09-23):**
    - 101 caches removed from 99 runs in 25 worktrees: 825.1 GB. The review's 768 GB figure predates the last day.
    - About four cold builds per worktree; the largest single removal was about 131 GB.
  - **Projection with one cache per worktree.** Removal volume is bounded by each worktree's largest cache: at most 219.3 GB over the same period, a 3.8× reduction. Build writes fall further, because later steps recompile only what changed.
  - **This does not reach the order-of-magnitude target by itself.** Sharing per repository would, but it serializes parallel worktrees on Cargo's lock and never lets the cache be removed. That choice is raised as an operator decision.
  - **Status:** partial until removal volume is re-measured on live data after deploy.
- **Amended 2026-09-24 (operator decision): keep one cache per worktree; restate the done-when.** No per-repository cache: it would serialize parallel worktrees on Cargo's lock and never free disk.
  - **New done-when:** after deploy, cache removal volume over a comparable ten-day window is re-measured against the 825 GB audit baseline (2026-09-13 to 2026-09-23), and the measured reduction is recorded here. The projection is at least 3.8×.
  - It is met by the measurement, whatever the figure. A per-repository cache is reconsidered only if the measured cut falls well short of the projection.
- **Amendment (2026-09-24 review):** the shared cache sits outside the worktree and the run directory. It was missing from the launch request's `additionalDirectories`, so Codex in workspace-write mode could not write to it, and every Codex Cargo build would have failed. It is now listed, and the launch test asserts it.

### R-G8

**Backend capability model and persistent-agent seam** · Phase P5 · Effort M-L · Status: open

- **Resolves:** [AGT-10](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-10-backend-capabilities-are-expressed-as-backend--codex-checks-scattered-across-daemon-domain-contracts-and-web), [AGT-11](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-11-the-seam-cannot-host-persistent-hermesopenclaw-style-agents-without-redesign)
- **Change:** Replace ~15 backend === "codex" checks (7 in the web) with declared capabilities; open the closed backend CHECK constraint; split transport from backend so a persistent (Hermes/OpenClaw-style) agent can host runs.
- **Done when:** Adding a third backend requires no web changes.

### R-G9

**Authentication and authorization hardening** · Phase P2 · Effort M · Status: open

- **Resolves:** [SEC-04](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#sec-04-authentication-hardening-is-weak-for-a-session-that-amounts-to-code-execution), [SEC-05](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#sec-05-route-authorization-depends-on-every-handler-remembering-to-call-it), [SEC-06](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#sec-06-the-browser-can-register-any-host-path-as-a-repository), [SEC-07](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#sec-07-missing-browser-security-headers-and-host-check), [SEC-08](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#sec-08-stored-credentials-are-readable-by-agents-and-old-db-copies-are-retained), [QA-03](findings/QA-DOC-REPO-tests-docs-hygiene.md#qa-03-the-authorization-surface-has-no-systematic-tests-and-the-no-approve-route-test-checks-spelling)
- **Change:** Login rate limiting, idle session expiry, step-up authentication for unrestricted runs and final promotion; a global route auth hook with an explicit public allowlist; repository registration limited to configured roots; security headers and Host check; credentials not readable from agent-writable locations; retire old DB copies.
- **Done when:** A route sweep test asserts 401/403/404 for every route and role.
- **Amended 2026-09-24 (operator decision, after R-I3):**
  - **Already done by R-I3 (2531715).** The route-guard part of the Change and the whole done-when:
    - every API route declares its access, and the daemon refuses to start without it;
    - one guard applies the declared checks before input validation;
    - `route-access.test.ts` asserts 401, 403 and 404 for every route and role.
  - **Added to the Change.** The guard attaches the authenticated context to the request, handlers read it, and the roughly 108 per-handler `authenticate`/`authorizeMutation` calls are deleted. QA-03 recommended this, and no item held it. The sweep is the safety net.
  - **Restated done-when:**
    - login is rate-limited;
    - sessions expire when idle;
    - unrestricted runs and final promotion require step-up authentication;
    - repository registration is limited to the configured roots;
    - security headers and a Host check are sent;
    - credentials are not readable from agent-writable locations, and old database copies are retired;
    - no route handler calls `authenticate` or `authorizeMutation`, and the route sweep still passes.

### R-G10

**Git adapter robustness and structure** · Phase P3 · Effort M · Status: open

- **Resolves:** [GIT-03](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#git-03-large-diffs-fail-outright-instead-of-truncating-and-the-diff-limit-setting-is-partly-ignored), [GIT-05](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#git-05-scratch-worktree-merges-check-out-the-whole-target-every-time-and-the-target-check-is-not-atomic), [GIT-06](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#git-06-git-timeouts-and-output-limits-are-hard-coded), [GIT-07](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#git-07-promotions-into-the-primary-checkout-are-brittle), [GIT-10](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#git-10-operationsts-is-one-1754-line-factory-behind-a-24-method-interface), [GIT-11](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#git-11-the-risky-git-paths-lack-tests)
- **Change:** Large diffs truncate instead of failing; configurable timeouts and output limits; cheaper and atomic scratch merges; resilient promotions; split operations.ts by concern; tests for the risky paths.
- **Done when:** Tests cover merge timeout, large diff, scratch merge target race.

### R-G11

**Supervisor loose ends** · Phase P3 · Effort S-M · Status: open

- **Resolves:** [AGT-02](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-02-restart-recovery-relies-entirely-on-systemd-no-process-identity-is-persisted), [AGT-16](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-16-smaller-supervisor-defects), [AGT-17](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-17-documentation-drift-in-the-agent-seam), [AGT-62](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-62-claude-background-task-notifications-create-streams-of-invalid-review-turns-and-status-churn)
- **Change:** Persist process identity for restart recovery; fix the smaller supervisor defects; stop Claude background-task notifications from producing invalid review turns; update agent seam docs.
- **Done when:** See the AGT report entries.

### R-G12

**(Future) agent runs that outlive the daemon** · Phase P5 · Effort L · Status: open

- **Resolves:** [AGT-02](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-02-restart-recovery-relies-entirely-on-systemd-no-process-identity-is-persisted), [AGT-11](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-11-the-seam-cannot-host-persistent-hermesopenclaw-style-agents-without-redesign)
- **Change:** Recorded 2026-09-23 as a future item, to be designed together with persistent (Hermes/OpenClaw-style) agents (R-G8). Give each run a small supervisor in its own transient systemd user unit that owns the agent process and its stdio, persists process identity, and appends normalized events to a durable per-run file or socket; the daemon becomes a client that re-attaches to live supervisors on start and replays their events from its last journal cursor. This removes restart as a source of interruption entirely (R-B9 only shortens and repairs it) and is the same decoupling a persistent agent needs. Preserve today's authority boundaries: process spawning stays in the listed adapter modules, cancellation and deadlines stay daemon-owned.
- **Done when:** A daemon restart during a live run loses no events and needs no resume; the design ADR covers persistent agents as well.

### R-G13

**Declared per-repository checks** · Phase P2 · Effort M · Status: partial (increments 1 to 3 and 5; 4 open)

- **Added 2026-09-28** (operator decision, after the R-G4 batch), for what R-G4 left open.
- **Resolves:** the rest of [AGT-08](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#agt-08-verification-exists-only-for-cargo-non-rust-repositories-get-no-controller-supplied-verification), and R-G4's residual gap on [SEC-01](findings/AGT-GIT-SEC-agents-git-security.md#sec-01-agents-can-forge-the-buildcheckcinative-receipts-that-gate-integration).
- **Why:** since R-G4 the daemon runs every check and records its receipt, but the agent still chooses the command, so `ct-check -- true` counts as a scoped check. Verification exists only for Cargo; other repositories get none from the controller.
- **Change:**
  - A repository, or the plan's verification policy, declares its check commands.
  - The daemon runs the declared commands itself, in the check units R-G4 built, at the gates that need them. It may also run them on the agent's request.
  - A scoped-check gate is met only by a receipt of a declared check, run on the gated commit. Commands the agent chooses stay available and stay supplemental.
  - Cargo becomes one declared check among others, not a special case.
- **Needs a design first:** where declarations live (repository file, binding or plan); who may change them, given an agent can edit repository files; migration for repositories that declare nothing.
- **Done when:** A scoped-check gate cannot be met by a command the agent chose, and a non-Rust repository can declare a check the daemon runs.
- **Design, 2026-09-29 (operator decision: option A, fail closed).**
  - **Where declarations live.** An immutable, operator-adopted check declaration per repository, stored by the daemon (a new record kind, schema 36, and an audit action; operator-approved as part of the option). The operator adopts it from a file in the repository, `.craftingtable/checks.json`, which the daemon reads with its own Git at a commit the operator names, never from an agent's branch or worktree. The file is only a proposal; the record is the authority.
  - **Rejected:** declaring checks in the plan or map format (a format change, and plan-bundle and manual runs have no map), and reading the file from the integration tip at launch (delegated merges let an agent weaken the file in one slice for the next).
  - **Undeclared repositories fail closed.** For runs prepared after the cutover, a scoped run in a repository with no adopted declaration does not start: it stops as `repository-checks-undeclared`, owned by the operator, whose exit is adopting a declaration. Records frozen earlier keep today's rule. WI and EXO each need one adoption after the deploy.
  - **What counts.** In scoped mode, a gate is met only by daemon-recorded receipts of the adopted checks, one per declared check, on the gated commit. The daemon takes the command from the manifest it verified at launch (`ct-check --declared <id>`), never from the agent's request. Commands the agent chooses still run, and stay supplemental.
  - **Definitions the agent can edit.** A declared check names the files that define it (its scripts). The daemon records their blob ids at the gated commit with the receipt, and a gate whose definitions differ from the adopted ones stops as `check-definition-changed`; the operator's exit is adopting the new definition.
  - **The boundary, stated.** Declared checks stop agents choosing the gate's command and changing its harness unseen. They do not stop agents weakening the tests themselves; that stays the reviewer's job.
  - **Increments:** (1) the record, adoption, the typed stops, declared receipts and the scoped gate; (2) Cargo as one declared check, for current-upstream gates; (3) the daemon runs declared checks itself when a review starts; (4) a check-only manifest for plan-bundle and non-Rust repositories (AGT-08); (5) the repository Checks panel beyond adoption, with receipts labelled declared or supplemental.
  - **Operator decision 2026-09-30: increment 5 next, then 4.** Increment 5 also takes LIVE-30's diagnostics: adoption warns when its source commit is not on a bound integration branch or its definitions differ from that branch's head, and `check-definition-changed` says whether the slice or its target changed the definition. Increment 4 comes when a plan-bundle or non-Rust repository is about to join a roadmap. Increment 5 must trust `origin` only on runs whose receipts the daemon records (L4 below).
  - **Operator decision 2026-09-30 (LIVE-30): definition changes are adopted at merge.** Increment 5 also adopts on merge. A review whose slice changes a declared check's definition files no longer stops as `check-definition-changed`. Its merge approval shows the definition diff, and the operator's approval of that merge records a new adoption at the merge commit; slices in flight then count against it, since a receipt matching a later adoption already counts. This applies only where a person approves the merge; the design is put to the operator before it is built, since adoption stops being a separate step. Beside it, an operator action in the WorldInterface repository: make `scripts/check-wi-domain-isolation.py` read every `conformance/*/coverage.json` rather than naming each scope, and keep `.craftingtable/checks.json` on `wi-fabric-2`, so most slices change no definition.
  - The design text above was committed with the LIVE-18 review fixes (1e422d5), not in a commit of its own.
- **Progress, 2026-09-29: increment 1.**
  - **The record.** Migration 0036 (schema 36) adds `repository_check_declarations`: one immutable row per adoption, versioned per repository, with no-update and no-delete triggers, and the audit action `repository-checks.adopted`. A declaration names each check's id, argv and definition files, the SHA-256 of each definition file at the adopted commit, the rationale, who adopted it and when. It is a new record kind, read and written through the R-H3 guard.
  - **Adoption.** The Repositories page has a Checks section. For each active repository it shows the adopted version and its checks, and earlier versions. An editor names a branch or commit; the daemon resolves it and reads `.craftingtable/checks.json` from that commit with its own Git (`resolveCommit`, `exportCommit`), never from a worktree. The preview lists the checks and any issues. Adoption records the commit the operator reviewed and is refused if the ref has moved since, or if the file has issues. The file's schema refuses absolute or `..` programs, options in place of a program, duplicate ids and missing definition files. A program with a path is the repository's own script and defines its check unless the file names other definition files.
  - **The gate.** A run in scoped-checks mode, prepared after this change, carries the repository's current adoption in its manifest (`declaredChecks`) and its record (`checkDeclarationId`). `ct-check --declared <id>` runs the adopted argv from the manifest the daemon verified at launch; the request names the check and nothing else, and extra arguments are refused. The daemon's receipt records the check, the adoption, and the SHA-256 of each definition file. (After the review below, the check runs in a private clone of the reviewed commit with a PATH the run cannot write, and the digests are of the stored blobs.) The review gate and the checkpoint candidate gate then need one successful, clean, daemon-recorded receipt of every adopted check on the reviewed commit. Commands the agent chooses, even the adopted command itself, still run and are recorded, and count for nothing. Runs prepared before this change keep the old rule. The checkpoint candidate gate applies the same rule to a run held to declared checks. No candidate is in scoped-checks mode today (a slice whose merge needs a contract checkpoint is always held to a current-upstream build), so that branch is defensive and has no test of its own; increment 2 exercises it.
  - **Typed stops.** A scoped run in a repository with no adoption does not start: its cycle stops as `repository-checks-undeclared`, owned by the operator, with the repository as a ref. A gate whose declared check ran only with other definition files (edited, or replaced by a link) stops as `check-definition-changed` with the repository and check. Both inbox items open the repository's checks (`/repositories#repository-checks-<id>`), where adopting is the exit.
  - **Tests.** `repository-checks.test.ts` (reads the named commit, not the working tree or another branch; versions; audit; moved ref and each kind of issue refused; triggers; member reads, editor writes, CSRF, and the service's own role check). `server-execution-receipt-gates.test.ts` (a chosen command, even the adopted one, does not meet the gate; extra arguments and unknown checks refused; the declared check meets it and its receipt names the adoption; an edited or linked definition stops as `check-definition-changed`; an undeclared repository stops as `repository-checks-undeclared` before any launch, and its item opens the repository's checks). Existing scoped tests now adopt fixture checks and run `ct-check --declared`. Web: `RepositoryChecksPanel.test.tsx`, and the repositories route with a focus.
  - **Guards shown by mutation**, each killed by the tests above: extra arguments accepted; any scoped receipt counted; definition digests not compared; an undeclared repository not failing closed; a linked definition followed; the undeclared stop untyped; adoption reading the working tree; a moved ref adopted; a file with issues adopted; the service's editor check replaced by membership; the repository program not resolved in the worktree.
  - **Walkthrough.** `2026-09-29-repository-checks-before` (0cf4fa5) and `-after` (8d0482c). The Repositories page gains the Checks section, one block per active repository; desktop grows from one screen (900 px) to 1,244 px, phone from 1,661 px to 2,257 px with two repositories.
  - **Operator action after deploy (approved 2026-09-29).** Scoped slices in WI and EXO stop as `repository-checks-undeclared` until each repository's checks are adopted. Each needs a `.craftingtable/checks.json` committed on a branch the operator reviews, then adoption on the Repositories page.
  - **Open:** increments 2 to 5. Plan-bundle runs and repositories without a pinned environment get no manifest yet, so they are not gated by declared checks until increment 4.
- **Independent review of 8d0482c (2026-09-29, isolated worktree).** The reviewer wrote proof tests and mutations against the commit, reading no live data. Three HIGH findings; every finding is fixed or dispositioned, and each fix has a test that fails without it (mutations R1 to R8 below, all killed).
  - *HIGH, fixed (R1):* the agent could replace the program a declared check runs. The check unit's PATH began with the run's launcher directory, which the agent (and any supplemental check) can write, so a planted `git` or `cargo`, or anything a script calls by name, ran instead. A declared check now gets a PATH without any directory the run can write (the run directory, the worktree, every writable path). Test: a planted `git` never runs and the real one does.
  - *HIGH, fixed (R2, R8):* the check ran in the agent's live worktree, which the agent can change while the check runs (it keeps working), and `git update-index --skip-worktree` hid such changes from the clean test; definition digests were read from the worktree after the run. A declared check now runs in a daemon-private clone of the reviewed commit (under the check log root, outside every writable root; it borrows the repository's objects and is removed after the check), with its own scratch and a daemon-owned Cargo target directory per run, removed when the run's checks close. Definition digests are the stored blobs at the reviewed commit, read before the check runs, as the design said. Tests: a result the agent writes in its worktree, hidden with skip-worktree, does not reach the check; an adopted script restored in the worktree over a weakened committed one still stops as `check-definition-changed`.
  - *HIGH, fixed (availability):* adoption exported the whole commit to read one file, and the export refuses any tree with a link, a submodule, more than 10,000 files or a file over 16 MB, answering "could not be read" with no reason. With fail-closed, such a repository could never adopt. Adoption now reads only the checks file and the definition files (`readCommitFiles`, new in the Git boundary: exact paths, stored bytes, a link reported as a link), and passes Git's reason through. Test: a repository with a link and a 17 MB file previews and adopts; a definition file that is a link is refused by name.
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (R4):* a tag named like the branch shadowed it (`main^{commit}` prefers `refs/tags/main`), and anyone who can write refs, agents included, can make one. Adoption now reads `refs/heads/<name>` or an exact commit, and says so when neither matches. Test: a tag `main` on a weaker file is ignored.
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (R5):* `check-definition-changed` never stopped a cycle: the gate ran only at merge, where a manual merge got a 409, and adopting the new definition could never clear it, because the run stays held to the adoption current at its launch. Now a review held to declared checks is tested when it is approved, so the cycle stops with the typed stop and its item opens the repository's checks. A receipt whose definitions match a later adoption of the same command counts, so adopting clears the stop: resume re-reviews, and the stopped review would merge as it is. The message now says to adopt and then resume, or to stop and revert in a new attempt. Test: implement edits the script, review stops as `check-definition-changed`, adoption, resume, awaiting merge.
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (R6):* naming definition files replaced a script's own path rather than adding to it, and a program on PATH with no definition files left what it reads (a Makefile, `package.json`) editable without a stop, silently. A repository program now always defines its check; a program file that is not executable is an issue; a PATH program with no definition files is a warning the preview shows, since some checks (`git diff --check`) read none. The operator decides.
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (R7):* the declared gate's `clean` and head conditions were untested. Tests: an untracked file while the check ran, and a commit before it, each leave the gate unmet.
  - *LOW, fixed:* the preview did not show what is adopted. It now shows each definition file's text (short UTF-8, first 64 KiB) and whether it is new, unchanged or changed since the current version.
  - *NIT, fixed:* repeated issue keys in the panel.
  - *NIT, disposition:* the stored-record schema is looser than the proposal schema about `argv[0]`; only adoption writes records, through the proposal schema. The finalization exemption in `prepare` is unreachable (finalization passes no scope), and is kept as an explicit statement of the rule. A preview for a missing or retired repository answers 400 like the other repository routes.
  - *Probes that found nothing:* receipts forged or from another run, runtime, manifest or declaration; `--declared` parsing; ref patterns; the moved-ref window; version races; role and CSRF checks; fail-closed escapes (a new run cannot look old); migration 0036; the undeclared stop and its resume.
  - *Mutations after the fixes:* R1 to R8 above are killed, and increment 1's are killed again against the new code (three rewritten for it: digests not compared, adoption digests read from the working tree, the program not resolved in the clone). The linked-definition mutant no longer applies: a link's stored blob is its target path, which never matches an adopted script.
  - *Scope of the approval test:* only a review held to declared checks is tested at approval, so runs prepared before this change behave as before.
- **Independent review of the fixes, 242bc20 (2026-09-29, isolated worktree).** The reviewer ran Git and Cargo experiments in scratch directories and read no live data. Two HIGH findings; one is fixed, and the other needs an operator decision. Each fix has a test that fails without it (mutations S1 to S9, all killed).
  - *HIGH, fixed after the operator's decision (2026-09-29, below):* the declared check still built from the daemon's Cargo home, which agents and every check unit can write (the R-G5 follow-up decision: agents and check units share `<data>/cargo-home`). Cargo never re-checks sources it has extracted, so an agent could edit `registry/src/.../lib.rs` of a dependency, through a supplemental `ct-check -- sh -c ...` or its own fetches, and any later build compiled the edit: the reviewer proved it with a planted `compile_error!`. This predated R-G13: every pinned Cargo gate since R-G5 read the same home.
  - *HIGH, fixed (S1):* the clone borrowed the shared object store (`--shared`), and Git never re-hashes an object it reads: an agent could rewrite the bytes of a loose object (a failing test file) during its check and restore them after. The clone is now `--no-local`, so objects travel through a pack that Git hashes on receipt, and a rewritten object fails the clone. The reviewed commit must be on a branch (the agent's slice branch); a detached commit fails closed. Test: a rewritten loose object makes the check fail with "could not be checked out".
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (S2):* the clone's directory was named by the request id, which the agent chooses, so a second request with the same id could replace a running check's tree. The daemon names it. Test: the clone's directory matches no request.
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (S3):* a committed link (or a Cargo directory source) could still reach the agent's live files, because `ProtectHome=read-only` allows reads. The declared unit now has `InaccessiblePaths=` for the worktree, the launcher directory and the run's writable roots other than the Cargo caches (`declaredUnitSettings`). Test on the unit's settings and arguments.
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (S4):* one Cargo target served every commit of a run, so a build script of an earlier, unreviewed commit could leave outputs the reviewed commit's check reuses. Each reviewed commit has its own. Test: the check sees `declared-target/<head>`.
  - *LOW, fixed (S5):* with no branch of that name, `refs/heads/<name>` fell back to other refs (`git tag refs/heads/x` is accepted). Adoption now resolves `refs/heads/<name>` exactly (`exactBranchCommit`, `show-ref --verify`). Test: a tag `refs/heads/ct/checks` is refused.
  - *LOW, fixed (S6):* replace refs changed what an object reads as. Every daemon Git command now sets `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS` (the Git boundary and the check runner). Test: a replaced definition file previews as committed.
  - *LOW, fixed (S7, S8, S9):* test gaps. Unit tests now cover `declaredCheckGaps` with a later adoption of another command, other paths or other digests; PATH filtering (entries that reach a writable root through a link are now resolved and refused); and cleanup, which also sweeps at start what a stopped daemon left.
  - *Probes that found nothing:* the clone's configuration, hooks, filters and submodules; the head race; `committedDigests` parsing; `readCommitFiles` limits and links; the exact-commit rule; the `later` comparison as written; the approval-time check (typed, resumable, no loop, other errors left to merge); cleanup never following links or touching logs.
- **Operator decision, 2026-09-29: a fresh, private Cargo home per check.** The operator approved the recommendation above, changing the R-G5 follow-up decision for check units only; agents still fetch into `<data>/cargo-home`.
  - **Checked first:** Cargo trusts a `.crate` it finds in its cache without checking it against the lock (a rewritten `itoa-1.0.18.crate` compiled), so the daemon checks each one itself.
  - **Change.** Every check unit that can run Cargo (`ct-check`, declared or not, and pinned `cargo` builds) gets its own `CARGO_HOME` under the check's private directory. It holds a copy of the registry index, the downloaded crates whose SHA-256 matches a checksum in the checked tree's `Cargo.lock` files (each copied, then hashed, so a later rewrite of the shared file cannot slip in), and each Git dependency's database cloned through a pack (`--no-local`), which Git hashes on receipt. Links and anything else are left out, as are extracted sources, checkouts, configuration and credentials. Cargo extracts sources afresh; a crate that is missing or rewritten is left out and named in the check's output, and the offline build fails. The unit cannot see the shared home (`InaccessiblePaths`), can write only its own, and the home is removed when the check ends. The approved native environment keeps the operator's Cargo home (ADR-054); `docs/security.md` and `docs/operations.md` say so.
  - **Tests** (`local-check.test.ts`): a lock's registry packages and checksums; a fresh home holds the index, the verified crate and a good Git database, and no rewritten or unpinned crate, forged Git database, extracted source, configuration, credentials, checkout or link, with the shared home untouched and the check's own removed; with real Cargo, a line planted in the shared home's extracted source breaks a build from the shared home and never the check's, and a rewritten download fails the check; under systemd, a supplemental check cannot read the shared home. Mutations C1 to C7 (shared home used, downloads not verified, sources copied, shared home visible, links followed, lock not read, Git database borrowed) each fail a test.
  - **Left for increment 2:** pinned `cargo` builds (the current-upstream gate) now use a private Cargo home too, but still run in the agent's live worktree with a Cargo target in the run's scratch directory, which the agent can write. Increment 2 makes Cargo one declared check, on the private clone and target.
- **Independent review of d141efe (2026-09-29, isolated worktree), and a second operator decision.** The reviewer ran real Cargo against copies of the operator's cache and read no live data. Three HIGH findings; each is fixed and has a test that fails without it (mutations K1 to K7, all killed).
  - *HIGH, fixed (K1):* any `Cargo.lock` in the tree could approve a planted crate. The crates to copy were read from every lock, last one winning, and verified against the lock's own claim: a committed `tests/fixtures/old/Cargo.lock` naming a planted `itoa` checksum got the planted crate compiled, and so did a TOML string that the hand-rolled parser read as a package.
  - *HIGH, fixed (K1):* the agent-writable index vouched for checksums. With a planted checksum in the root lock and one edited line in the index's `.cache` entry, Cargo compiled the planted crate.
  - **Operator decision, 2026-09-29: checksums come from crates.io over HTTPS.** Options put to the operator: crates.io's own index over HTTPS (recommended), the operator's `~/.cargo` index only, or trust on first use. The daemon now reads each locked crate's published checksum from `https://index.crates.io/` (`CratesIoChecksums`), keeps it in `<data>/crate-checksums.json` for good, and copies a crate only if its bytes match. Whichever lock names a crate, and whatever the index says, only published bytes reach the check. No other registry is trusted; an unreachable index leaves a new crate unverified and the check fails closed. This is the daemon's first outbound request of its own. `docs/operations.md` and `docs/security.md` say so.
  - *HIGH, fixed (K2):* an agent could freeze the daemon: files were checked, then copied synchronously, so one swapped for a FIFO blocked the event loop for good. Every copy now opens without following links and without blocking, checks the open file, reads within a size cap, and runs asynchronously; the index copy has file and byte limits.
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (K3):* every Git database was cloned for every check, with no deadline. Only databases holding a commit a lock pins are cloned now, and preparation stops at the check's deadline or cancellation.
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (K5):* the private home's path was new for every check, and Cargo's fingerprints name it, so every check rebuilt every dependency. Each concurrent check of a run has a stable home path (`cargo-home-<n>`); the home is still made fresh. Test: a second check compiles nothing.
  - *MEDIUM, disposition (K4):* a tree without a committed lock, or with a format-1 lock, gets no registry crates, so its offline checks fail. The check now says so, and `docs/operations.md` names it. Generating a lock in the daemon would resolve versions from the agent-writable index; committing the lock is the fix.
  - *LOW, disposition:* pinned `cargo` builds read locks from the live worktree before recording their start state. The lock now only chooses which crates to copy, never which bytes, so this cannot let a planted crate in; the live worktree itself is increment 2's.
  - *LOW, fixed:* tests for several locks, a planted crate vouched for by its own lock and by the index, an unverifiable crate, a FIFO, a link, a size cap, Git databases no lock names, a tree without a lock, the checksum authority (layout, persistence, other registries, an unreachable index).
  - *Residual, stated:* the index copy is still metadata agents can write. Cargo reads features and dependencies from it; a poisoned entry can turn on features of genuine crates or break resolution, not change their bytes.
  - *Probes that found nothing:* a malicious bare repository in `git/db` runs nothing at clone time; paths cannot escape the private home; the wiring covers pinned metadata and builds, supplemental and declared checks; the home is removed on every exit and swept at start.
- **Independent review of 4aaa344 (2026-09-29, isolated worktree).** The reviewer ran real Cargo 1.98.1 in network-less namespaces and read no live data. Two HIGH findings, both through the copied index; both are fixed, and every guard is shown by mutation (N3 to N6 and K1, K3 to K6 again, all killed).
  - *HIGH, fixed:* the index copy carried agent-written bytes to a predictable path. A planted directory under the shared index plus a committed `.cargo/config.toml` that replaced crates.io with it compiled planted code with `--offline --locked` and the genuine lock.
  - *HIGH, fixed:* without `--offline`, the copied index's `config.json` could set `dl` to a local file, from which Cargo "downloaded" a planted crate.
  - **The fix for both:** the shared index is no longer copied at all. The check's home is a local registry the daemon writes: the crate's published index file, kept by the authority from crates.io (`indexFile`), and the matching downloads, with a `config.toml` replacing crates.io by it; the unit runs with `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true`, which the tree's configuration cannot override. A committed Cargo configuration outranks the home's, so this alone did not confine what it can point at; see the next review. This also closes the residual stated earlier: index metadata now comes from crates.io too. Cargo itself checks each local-registry crate against its index line (checked by experiment). Tests: a planted vendor directory under the shared index, with a committed configuration pointing at it, does not build; a build asked to go online fetches nothing; the home holds `ct-verified/` and its configuration, and no `registry/` at all. Mutations: not replacing crates.io, and allowing Cargo online, each fail a test.
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (moot):* the index walk counted only files. There is no index walk now.
  - *LOW, fixed (moot):* a crate could land in the wrong registry directory. The local registry is flat.
  - *LOW, fixed (N4):* failed lookups were retried on every check; a crate that could not be learned is not asked about again for ten minutes. Lookups are also bounded by the check's deadline.
  - *LOW, fixed:* one Git clone could overrun the deadline by up to two minutes; each Git step now gets the remaining time as its limit. Cancelling a check still waits for a running Git step to end (bounded by that limit).
  - *NIT, fixed (N5):* the 64 MiB limit was checked after reading the whole response; it is read as it arrives and cut off at the limit. (An earlier version of this mutation fed an endless stream, which exhausted memory once the limit was removed and took the session's terminal down; the test now sends a finite body past the limit.)
  - *NIT, disposition:* memory per crate is bounded at 256 MiB and checks run at most four per run and eight in the daemon.
  - *Disposition, corrected by the next review:* the review suggested counting `.cargo/config*` as definition files of declared checks that use Cargo. The next review showed agent-writable paths were still in reach; hiding them all is the fix.
- **Independent review of d1c2fdc (2026-09-29, isolated worktree).** Real Cargo in network-less namespaces; no live data. Two HIGH findings, both fixed and shown by mutation (V1 to V3, V5, all killed).
  - *HIGH, fixed (V1):* the tree's own `.cargo/config.toml` outranks the home's, and Cargo reads `source` from no environment variable, so a committed `[source.crates-io] replace-with` to a directory, `paths` overrides, `[patch]`, `[env]`, a linker or rustc wrapper, a `rust-toolchain.toml` path, or a committed link, could point at any path the unit could read. The declared unit hid only this run's roots; other runs' worktrees and run directories, the worktree build caches and the CI cache stayed readable, and agents fill them after review. The declared unit now mounts an empty read-only file system over every root agents write, of any run (the data directory, the run and worktree roots, the shared Cargo home, the CI cache), and binds back only its clone, scratch, build outputs and Cargo home (`TemporaryFileSystem=`, `BindPaths=`; systemd refuses to reopen a path under `InaccessiblePaths=`, checked by experiment). The tree's configuration can then reach only the reviewed clone, the check's own paths, and the operator's system and toolchain. Test (under systemd): a declared check cannot read another run's file under the data directory and can write its own clone.
  - *HIGH, fixed (V3):* locks were read synchronously, following links, with no count or size limit: a FIFO, a link to `/dev/zero` or two thousand copies of a large lock could block or exhaust the daemon. Locks are read with the safe reader, at most 64 of them and 64 MiB in all. Test: a FIFO lock neither blocks nor fails the check.
  - *MEDIUM, fixed (V2):* every locked crate was looked up, downloaded or not, and every lookup rewrote one ever-growing file. Only downloaded crates are looked up, at most 5,000 per check; each crate's index file is its own file, written once per lookup, and the checksums are read from it; remembered failures are bounded. Test: a crate never downloaded is not looked up.
  - *LOW, disposition:* pinned `cargo` builds and supplemental checks still run in the live worktree, where the tree's configuration can point at agent-written files. Supplemental checks count for nothing; pinned builds move onto declared checks in increment 2. `docs/security.md` no longer claims more.
  - *LOW, disposition:* a pinned upstream that adds a crates.io dependency missing from the consumer's lock now fails offline resolution with the pinned build's constraints message; the lock must carry it, as `--locked` already required.
  - *LOW, disposition:* a Git dependency's locked commit is verified to be the bytes its name says, not to come from the dependency's remote; `docs/operations.md` says so.
  - *LOW, fixed (V5):* nothing tested a crate name with capitals. Test: `Mixed` is copied with its published name and indexed at `mi/xe/mixed`; no index file is written for a crate that did not match.
  - *Probes that found nothing:* `+build` versions and yanked versions build from the local registry; names and paths cannot collide or escape; saved index files hold validated lines only; `<data>/crates-io` is written by no agent or unit; the check's environment carries no agent `CARGO_*` variable; pinned patches still resolve offline under the replacement; every lock format works, since checksums come from crates.io.
- **Independent review of e9ca92c and eda3b86 (2026-09-29, isolated worktree).** Two HIGH findings in the hidden roots, both fixed; each guard is shown by mutation (W1 to W8; W2 is equivalent in the test, below).

The declared unit now hides the operator's whole home and binds back, read-only, only the Rust toolchain (`RUSTUP_HOME`) and the trusted PATH directories under it; its `HOME` is its scratch directory. The repository's own Git directory is also hidden explicitly, for repositories outside the home (W2 survives in the test only because the fixture repository is under the home).   - *R-C3b hold, LOW, fixed:* the per-pass inbox could go stale while the pass awaited launches; it is rebuilt whenever workflow state changed since (the notifier's workflow generation). No test of its own.
  - *R-C3b hold, LOW, fixed (W8):* no test covered a clause-limited brief that names the held slice. Test: it holds.

  - *HIGH, fixed (X1):* every check unit could reach the user's runtime directory, whose user bus starts units outside every confinement: `systemd-run --user` from inside a check listed the whole home (confirmed here too). - **Found while drafting WI's and EXO's checks files (2026-09-30):** the declared check's clone was made with `--no-tags`, but EXO's contract check (`scripts/check_exo_v3_contract.py`) reads the tag `exoskeleton/pre-exo-v3` and compares it with a fixed commit, so it would always have failed. Tags now come along through the same verified pack; a moved tag fails such a comparison closed. Test: a declared check reads a tag in its clone (it failed before the change).

- **First boot after the e0d33b8 deploy (2026-09-30 snapshot, 02:14 UTC).** Schema 36 migrated at 00:31:12, no daemon warnings. WI's (version 1, ecf7cd18, 4 checks) and EXO's (version 1, ee20f2df, 7 checks) adoptions recorded at 00:34. Six runs held to a declaration, but no receipt names a declared check: LIVE-23 below. `<data>/crates-io/` holds 185 index files; finished runs' `check-logs/<run>/` keep only logs (scratch remains only under the three live runs). No `repository-checks-undeclared` or `check-definition-changed` stop.
- **Increment 2 design, decided by the operator 2026-09-30.** Today a pinned `cargo` build (the current-upstream gate) runs in the agent's live worktree, with a target, a pins file and upstream exports the agent can write (checked by digest only before and after the build), and the agent chooses the arguments, so `cargo check -p <trivial>` meets the gate. Options put to the operator: (A) run the agent's pinned request on private clones only; (B) on A's plumbing, hold current-upstream gates to the adopted checks too, run with the pins; (C) a built-in adopted `cargo` check (a checks-file and schema change). The operator chose B on A's plumbing, and that `exportCommit`'s unverified read of pinned sources is replaced in the first commit.
  - **A fact that changed the gate, and a second decision the same day.** WI's adopted checks all build `crates/worldinterface-foundation`, which does not depend on ActionQueue, so none resolves an AQ pin; a rule that an adopted check must resolve a pinned crate would stop every WI current-upstream review after the deploy. The operator chose: the gate needs every adopted check, run by the daemon on the private clone with the pins, **and** one daemon-run pinned `cargo` build on the private clone that resolves a pinned crate (the agent still chooses that build's arguments, but cannot skip or replace the adopted checks). No new receipt field is needed: a receipt's manifest digest already binds the pins.
  - **Commit 1: private clones for pinned builds, and verified pinned sources.**
    - `exportCommit` (the Git boundary) reads an exact commit through a pack: it fetches the commit by id (`--depth=1`, any commit, reachable or not) into a daemon-private repository, which Git hashes on receipt, and with a pin's tree it checks that too. Prepare, runtime pins and historical baselines all export this way.
    - Prepare records where each supplied upstream comes from in the run's manifest (`dependencySources`: alias, commit, tree, the upstream's Git directory and package paths). A new manifest field in the run directory, not a persisted record; runs prepared before it keep building as before.
    - The daemon runs a pinned build of a clean worktree on a private `--no-local` clone of the committed head, against private checkouts of each pinned commit (fetched by id through a pack, trees checked, package paths confined to their source) and a pins file it writes, bound read-only; the target is `declared-target/<commit>`; the unit is the declared checks' (every root agents write hidden, the upstreams' Git directories too). The receipt names the packages as the manifest does. A build of uncommitted work, which no gate accepts, still runs in the worktree: the agent's own loop.
    - **Tests:** `operations.test.ts` (a commit no branch names exports; another tree is refused; a rewritten loose object fails); `local-check.test.ts` (the build's directory, target, committed file and upstream bytes are the private ones while the worktree hides a change with skip-worktree; uncommitted work builds in the worktree with `clean: false`; a wrong tree, a rewritten upstream object and a package path outside its source each fail; under systemd, the unit reads the pinned sources read-only and sees no data, worktree or run copy); `server-execution-upstream-transitions.test.ts` (a real current-upstream `cargo test` builds through the private clones, and the manifest names the pin's source).

Three are equivalent in the tests: C5 (the pinned directory also listed writable: the read-only bind wins); C8 (reading the source store after the fetch: a rewritten object already fails the fetch, and reading the private copy closes the window between the two); C9 (prepare passing no tree: a fetched commit's id already fixes its tree, and the check verifies the tree again).

- **R-G13 posture, decided by the operator 2026-09-30** (the verification review's open items): (1) the daemon runs the checked commit's rustup-managed toolchain itself, refuses any other (a `path` toolchain included), and sets the compiler and wrappers; (2) pinned builds override the tree's wrapper, linker, runner and alias settings, and every adopted check that runs Cargo names the commit's Cargo configuration and toolchain files as definition files. Finalization runs are held to adopted checks too (operator decision, same day).
  - **Adoption.** A check that runs Cargo (as its program, or in a shell string; `argvRunsCargo`, shared with the check runner) is refused at adoption unless it names each of `.cargo/config.toml`, `.cargo/config`, `rust-toolchain.toml` and `rust-toolchain` its commit has. **WI's and EXO's fmt checks (and WI's isolation check) do not name `.cargo/config.toml`, so both repositories need a re-adoption.** Test: `repository-checks.test.ts`. Mutations P1 (rule off) and P2 (shell strings not seen), killed.

That toolchain's `bin` goes first on PATH and names `RUSTC` and `RUSTDOC`, and every rustc wrapper variable is set empty, which outranks the tree's `build.rustc-wrapper` (checked by experiment). Without rustup, the daemon's Cargo and its neighbours are the toolchain.

The review gate now requires the adopted checks of any run held to them, even where no build is required (a current-upstream review with no pinned upstream, or a finalization of such a map), where it used to return before any receipt; the pinned-build requirement still applies only where it did.
